1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	\looseness=-1
5	Formal verification's value depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. SICA reduces to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape; symbolic confirmation bias holds across nine models (7B to frontier). Three zero-cost diagnostics predict failure from a $K{=}12$ pilot (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}). Cross-architecture verification escapes only when the verifier exceeds generator accuracy (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}), suggesting \emph{architectural diversity} over scale. Trained verifiers---PRM \citep{lightman2024prm,wang2024mathshepherd}, ORM \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}---succeed by breaking the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ chain with external signal; our NLI escape instantiates this without task-specific training ($+6.67$\,pp). Lightweight trained verification is a natural next step (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
6

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	\label{sec:main-results}
4	
5	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
6	\label{sec:exp-setup}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	\label{sec:pipeline}
4	\label{sec:condorcet}
5	\label{sec:effective-k}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}), the effective ensemble size $K_\text{eff}$ is
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = K \,/\, [1 + (K{-}1) \cdot \kappa]
11	\end{equation}
12	(following \citet{ladha1992correlated}). Intuitively, $K_\text{eff}$ measures how many effectively independent votes $K$ correlated traces contribute; e.g., $K{=}12$ traces with $\kappa{=}0.69$ yield $K_\text{eff} \approx 1.3$, meaning twelve correlated traces carry no more independent information than 1.3 uncorrelated ones. This motivates constraint-based aggregation.
13	
14	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
15	
16	\begin{figure*}[t]
17	\centering
18	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{f2_regime_scatter_v2}
19	\caption{SICA $\Delta$ (pp) vs.\ Fleiss' $\kappa$ (inter-trace agreement). Color encodes model family; shape encodes dataset (\scalebox{0.7}{$\bullet$}\,PW, \scalebox{0.7}{$\blacktriangle$}\,FOLIO, \scalebox{0.7}{$\blacksquare$}\,LogiQA). Error bars show multi-seed standard deviation. High $\kappa$ ($K_{\text{eff}} \to 1$) clusters near $\Delta \approx 0$; only the low-$\kappa$ region admits nonzero effects, consistent with the independence bottleneck.}
20	\label{fig:regime-scatter}
21	\end{figure*}
22	
23	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
24	
25	\looseness=-1

{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
  "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nSelf-Consistency (SC) improves large language model (LLM) reasoning by majority voting over chain-of-thought traces. A natural extension extracts formal logical constraints from traces and aggregates them with a symbolic solver, but we show this is a \\emph{symbolic mirage}. We test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints under zero-shot prompting and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT. Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer rather than providing independent verification, a phenomenon we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB). Formally, when nearly all constraints pass satisfiability checking, constraint-based aggregation degenerates to majority voting. This bias persists across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, with bias ratios of $1.66$--$4.97\\times$. Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck; a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges where methods that reduce trace correlation also destroy accuracy. Among training-free approaches, only a structurally independent cross-architecture verifier restores corrective signal; the bottleneck lies in self-extraction itself, not model capability. We provide three zero-cost diagnostics that predict failure modes from a pilot run\\footnote{Code and data are available at \\url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/symb_invariant_consensus-2E1F/}.}.\n\\end{abstract}\n"
}
```

  "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\\looseness=-1\nFormal verification's value depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. SICA reduces to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape; symbolic confirmation bias holds across nine models (7B to frontier). Three zero-cost diagnostics predict failure from a $K{=}12$ pilot (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nFor the broader neuro-symbolic community, our results expose a structural limitation of self-extraction pipelines: when the same model generates both reasoning and formal constraints, the symbolic layer inherits the model's distributional prior rather than providing an independent check. This limitation is not specific to logical constraint extraction; it applies to any self-verification paradigm where the verifier shares the generator's knowledge boundary \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nCross-architecture verification escapes only when the verifier exceeds generator accuracy (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); this points to \\emph{architectural diversity} over scale as the key design principle. Trained verifiers---PRM \\citep{lightman2024prm,wang2024mathshepherd}, ORM \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}---succeed precisely because they break the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ chain with independently supervised signal; our NLI escape instantiates this principle without task-specific training ($+6.67$\\,pp). For test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}, our findings imply that allocating additional inference budget to self-consistent sampling yields diminishing returns once $K_\\text{eff}$ saturates; redirecting compute toward structurally independent verification offers a more efficient path. Lightweight trained verification is a natural next step (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n"
}
```

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	\label{sec:main-results}
4	
5	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
6	\label{sec:exp-setup}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
14	
15	\subsection{Main Results}
16	
17	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. 55	\looseness=-1 Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure. Fleiss' $\kappa$ ranges from 0.19 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.94$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\ref{tab:independence}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\leq 40\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\geq 60\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\,pp despite $\kappa \in [0.45, 0.46]$. We trace the mechanism in \S\ref{sec:analysis}.
56	
57	\looseness=-1 We use TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp, justified on four grounds: (1)~on FOLIO ($n{=}204$), $\pm$2\,pp corresponds to ${\sim}$4 problems, near single-item influence; (2)~cross-seed $\sigma = 2.70$\,pp (Mistral-7B), placing $\pm$2\,pp below noise; (3)~the observed $|\Delta|$ median across 11 conditions is 1.47\,pp, so $\pm$2\,pp captures the central tendency; (4)~sensitivity analysis confirms $\pm$2\,pp as the narrowest discriminating window ($\pm$1\,pp: zero conditions pass; $\pm$3\,pp: all pass; Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). Four of 11 conditions pass TOST equivalence (minimum detectable effect (MDE) $\leq 2$\,pp); the remaining seven are underpowered (MDE $4.6$--$4.8$\,pp) but provide consistent directional evidence ($|\Delta| \leq 4.41$\,pp, none significant; Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Multi-seed replication: mean $\Delta = +0.16 \pm 2.70$\,pp across three Mistral-7B seeds (Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}); four Qwen2.5-14B seeds yield $\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp; four Qwen3-14B seeds

[Director 指令] **新增 Diagnostics Flowchart + 全文 display items 分散排版**

两个任务：

### 任务 A：制作 §5.3 Diagnostics 的 decision flowchart（Figure 5）

用 TikZ 制作一个 decision flowchart，展示 3 个零成本诊断指标的使用流程：

```
输入: K=12 pilot run
    ↓
[C₀ < 5%?] → Yes → "Degenerate: SICA ≡ SC, skip"
    ↓ No
[K_eff ≤ 1.30?] → Yes → "Low diversity: no corrective signal"
    ↓ No
[BR > 1 + SC regime?]
    → Type A (SC ≥ 60%): "Confirmation bias: SICA degrades"
    → Type C (SC ≤ 40%): "Error amplification"
    → Intermediate: "Potential gain (verify with held-out)"
```

设计要求：
- 单栏宽度 `figure[t]`，`width=\columnwidth`
- 节点用圆角矩形（判断）和矩形（结论）
- 配色与论文其他图一致（蓝/灰色系）
- Caption 自包含，说明这是 practitioners 的 a priori 诊断流程
- Label: `\label{fig:diagnostic-flowchart}`
- 放在 analysis.tex 的 §5.3 Diagnostic Framework 段落之后

### 任务 B：全文 display items 分散排版

目标：**除第 1 页外，每一页都有至少一个 figure 或 table**。

当前 7 个 display items（加上新 Figure 5 共 7 个）：
- Figure 1 (pipeline): p2 ✓
- Figure 2 (regime scatter): p4 ✓
- Table 1 (main results): p6
- Figure 3 (causal DAG): ?
- Figure 4 (kappa-accuracy): ?
- Figure 5 (diagnostic flowchart): 新增
- Table 2 (cross-generator NLI): ?

目标分布（理想状态，尽量接近）：
- p1: 无（只有 title/abstract）
- p2: Figure 1 ✓
- p3: 需要一个 display item（目前是纯文本 Related Work + Method）
- p4: Figure 2 ✓
- p5: 需要一个 display item
- p6: 一个 display item
- p7: 一个 display item

策略：
1. 调整 float 定义位置来控制落页
2. Table 1 是 `table*` 双栏宽，体积大，可以锚定某一页
3. Figure 3 和 Figure 5 都是单栏，可以灵活放置
4. 如果某页实在放不下，可以考虑把 Figure 4 改成 `figure*` 全宽
5. 反复编译微调直到分布满意

**编号约束**：Figure 编号顺序必须和文中首次引用顺序一致。

### 编译验证
- 每页（p2-p7/p8）都有至少一个 display item
- 正文 ≤ 8 页
- 0 undefined reference
- Figure/Table 编号顺序正确

完成后汇报每个 display item 的最终页数。

1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\geq}99\%$ of problems (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (three primary generators plus six additional models; BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$; Appendix~\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).
8	
9	\smallskip\noindent
10	\textbf{Observation 1} \textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\;%
11	Let $t_1, \ldots, t_K \overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim} P_M(\cdot \mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\{i : a_i = a\}|$.
12	Under (i)~the degeneration condition $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\arg\max_a \operatorname{score}(a) = \arg\max_a n_a$.
13	
14	\smallskip\noindent
15	\textit{Empirical support.}\;%
16	Z3 rejection rate $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}|/|\mathcal{C}| \approx 0.034$ confirms $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appen

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	\label{sec:main-results}
4	
5	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
6	\label{sec:exp-setup}
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
14	
15	\subsection{Main Results}
16	
17	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. 1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on ${\geq}99\%$ of problems (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}); the formal verification layer is thus vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The pattern generalizes across nine models from 7B to frontier (three primary generators plus six additional models; BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$; Appendix~\ref{app:frontier-scb}) and all cells of a $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix (Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}). A permutation test confirms BR reflects trace-count geometry, not extraction bias (Appendix~\ref{app:br-permutation}); controls rule out answer leakage (Appendix~\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).
8	
9	\smallskip\noindent
10	\textbf{Observation 1} \textit{(Argmax Preservation).}\;%
11	Let $t_1, \ldots, t_K \overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim} P_M(\cdot \mid q)$ with trace-determined answers $a_i = h(t_i)$ and $n_a = |\{i : a_i = a\}|$.
12	Under (i)~the degeneration condition $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), (ii)~source-attribution scoring where $\sigma(c,a) {=} 1$ iff $c$ originates from a trace producing $a$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:score}), and (iii)~approximately uniform constraint yield per trace, SICA reduces to majority vote: $\arg\max_a \operatorname{score}(a) = \arg\max_a n_a$.
13	
14	\smallskip\noindent
15	\textit{Empirical support.}\;%
16	Z3 rejection rate $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}|/|\mathcal{C}| \approx 0.034$ confirms $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appen
…[truncated 3714 chars]

[Director 指令] **继续优化 display items 分布——Table 1 前移到 p5**

用户要求除第 1 页外每页都有图或表。当前 p3 和 p5 仍是纯文本。

Table 编号和 Figure 编号是独立计数器，移动 Table 不影响 Figure 顺序。

**操作：**

1. 将 Table 1 (`table*`) 的定义在 experiments.tex 中尽量前移（放在 §4.2 Main Results 开头、正文首次引用之前），让 LaTeX 把它浮动到 p5 顶部（Table 1 首次被引用的页面）

8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias on six additional models: gpt-4o uses self-extraction via API; four frontier models (gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) use cross-model extraction where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B likewise uses Mistral-7B cross-extraction (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$), StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$). GSM8K ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s (K-sensitivity and extraction-temperature analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}). McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples; Appendix~\ref{app:bootstrap}) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
14	
15	\subsection{Main Results}
16	
17	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. Two confirmatory conditions show significant degradation after Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) FDR control ($m{=}6$; multi-seed aggregates and extensions are exploratory): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$, adj.\ $p = 0.042$) and Qwen2.5-14B on Pro
…[truncated 873 chars]

p8: Formal verification’s value depends on the
p8: independence of its inputs, not the soundness of

p8: source-attribution scoring (Observation 1), and
p8: 27 training-free variants fail to escape; symbolic
p8: confirmation bias holds across nine models (7B
p8: to frontier). Three zero-cost diagnostics predict
p8: failure from a K=12 pilot (§5.3).
p8: For the broader neuro-symbolic community,
p8: our results expose a structural limitation of
p8: self-extraction pipelines: when the same model
p8: generates both reasoning and formal constraints,
p8: the symbolic layer inherits the model’s distributional prior rather than providing an independent
p8: check. This limitation is not specific to logical constraint extraction; it applies to any self-verification
p8: paradigm where the verifier shares the generator’s
p8: knowledge boundary (Huang et al., 2024).
p8: Cross-architecture verification escapes only
p8: when the verifier exceeds generator accuracy
p8: (§5.4); this points to architectural diversity
p8: over scale as the key design principle. Trained
p8: verifiers—PRM (Lightman et al., 2024; Wang
p8: et al., 2024), ORM (Cobbe et al., 2021)—succeed
p8: precisely because they break the M →T →A→C
p8: chain with independently supervised signal; our
p8: NLI escape instantiates this principle without
p8: task-specific training (+6.67 pp). For test-time
p8: compute scaling (Snell et al., 2025), our findings
p8: imply that allocating additional inference budget to

p8: once Keff saturates; redi

Diversity-seeking strategies shift $K_\text{eff}$ by $< 0.5$; constraint-level interventions leave degeneration intact; debate ($\kappa = 0.15$, $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}); cross-model extraction achieves the lowest non-destructive $\kappa$ (${\approx}0.40$--$0.47$) but remains above the independence threshold ($+0.49$\,pp, n.s.; Appendix~\ref{app:intervention-individual}).
33	
34	\subsection{Diagnostic Framework}
35	\label{sec:diagnostic-framework}
36	\label{sec:diagnostics}
37	
38	\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict the outcome of any self-extraction-based aggregation from a $K{=}12$ pilot run (Figure~\ref{fig:diagnostic-flowchart}): (1)~degenerate-regime indicator $C_0 < 5\%$ (SICA reduces to SC); (2)~diversity floor $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$ (insufficient vote diversity); (3)~bias ratio BR combined with SC regime (Type~A: SC $\geq 60\%$, BR $> 1$ $\to$ confirmation-bias degradation; Type~C: SC $\leq 40\%$ $\to$ error amplification). These boundaries correctly characterize failure modes (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}) across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm.
39	
40	\looseness=-1 Decision boundaries were derived from Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B and validated prospectively on held-out generators (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B), correctly predicting Type~A bias ($\Delta = -4.41$\,pp) and non-significance ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Conversely, when diagnostics detect sufficient capability gap (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}), the framework correctly predicts positive gain ($+6.67$\,pp for Mistral-7B

[Director 指令] **修复 Figure 5 Diagnostics Flowchart：底部三个方框间距**

用户反馈 Figure 5 最下方的三个结论方框（Type A / Type C / Intermediate）重叠了，需要拉开间距。

修改 TikZ 代码：
1. 找到最底层的三个节点（应该是 BR+SC regime 判断后的三个结论方框）
2. 增大它们之间的水平间距（调整 `xshift` 或节点之间的距离参数）
3. 如果单栏宽度不够放三个并排方框，可以：
   - 减小方框内文字字号（`\scriptsize` 或 `\footnotesize`）
   - 缩短文字描述
   - 调整节点宽度（`text width` 参数）
   - 或改为纵向排列（三个方框竖着排，不并排）
4. 确保方框之间有明显的视觉间隔（至少 2-3mm）

编译后验证方框不再重叠，整体布局清晰。

当前第 2 段（line 13-14 of introduction.tex）太像实验记录。重写为高层叙事，删除实现细节：

**删除：**
- "prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver"
- "Four of 11 conditions confirm equivalence within ±2pp (Two One-Sided Tests; TOST); the remaining seven show consistent directional evidence but are underpowered for formal confirmation"
- "Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints [cf.] chan2026position"

**保留/改写为：**
简洁地说：我们用 SICA 测试了约束提取 → 结果在所有条件下 SICA ≈ SC，没有显著正效果 → 过渡到第 3 段的 why。大约 3-4 句话即可。参考结构：
1. 我们通过 SICA 测试这个直觉（简介方法，不展开技术细节）

3. 没有条件展示显著正效果（一句话总结，不列统计检验细节）

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces, as in neuro-symbolic approaches \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}, and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\begin{figure*}[t!]
7	\centering
8	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}
9	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
10	\label{fig:pipeline}
11	\end{figure*}
12	
13	\looseness=-1
14	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic (FOL); 99\% are propositional at the level of the Z3 SMT solver) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints \citep[cf.][]{chan2026position}, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).

_source line 7711 · 2026-05-25T12:21:16.602042+00:00_

9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	Three paradigms exist: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} and \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} formalize before answer generation; \emph{trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent work extends symbolic planning and verified reasoning \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable. Prior studies note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show they are \emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer (Observation~1). This reveals a mechanism underlying \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s finding that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback: we formalize the failure via the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain and provide three zero-cost diagnostics that predict failure modes before deployment. Our symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is distinct from CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2023language, jhaveri2026failing}: CoT bias concerns trace-level faithfulness, while SCB shows the \emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution even when individual traces are locally faithful.
11

_source line 7713 · 2026-05-25T12:21:19.163093+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Self-Consistency (SC) improves large language model (LLM) reasoning by majority voting over chain-of-thought traces. A natural extension extracts formal logical constraints from traces and aggregates them with a symbolic solver, but we show this is a \emph{symbolic mirage}. We test this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic constraints under zero-shot prompting and solves weighted partial MAX-SAT. Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm their source trace's answer rather than providing independent verification, a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB). Formally, when nearly all constraints pass satisfiability checking, constraint-based aggregation degenerates to majority voting. This bias persists across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, with bias ratios of $1.66$--$4.97\times$. Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies fail to break this bottleneck; a persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges where methods that reduce trace correlation also destroy accuracy. Among training-free approaches, only a structurally independent cross-architecture verifier restores corrective signal; the bottleneck lies in self-extraction itself, not model capability. We provide three zero-cost diagnostics that predict failure modes from a pilot run\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/symb_invariant_consensus-2E1F/}.}.
3	\end{abstract}
4

49	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\{q : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}| / |\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\pm}0.49$\,pp across $\alpha \in [0, 1]$).

_source line 7743 · 2026-05-25T12:22:21.919093+00:00_

1554	\paragraph{SCB diagnostic toolkit.}
1555	\label{app:diagnostic-toolkit}
1556	We recommend four zero-cost diagnostics, computable from sampled traces alone, to predict whether symbolic self-verification will help or hurt before deployment.
1557	\emph{Bias Ratio} (BR; \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}): for each problem, compute the fraction of constraints supporting the majority answer, divided by majority vote share. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints are biased toward the majority; $\text{BR} \gg 2$ (as in all nine models tested; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) signals that extraction amplifies rather than checks the model's prior. Report as mean $\pm$ bootstrap 95\% CI.
1558	\emph{Effective-K} ($K_\text{eff}$; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}): compute Fleiss' $\kappa$ across $K$ traces, then $K_\text{eff} = 1 + (K-1)(1-\kappa)$. Threshold: $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.30$ never improves in our experiments---aggregation collapses to majority voting. $K_\text{eff} > 1.8$ is a necessary (not sufficient) condition for gain.

66	\looseness=-1 Across 108 conditions, gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy (Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}), a preliminary \emph{capability-gap pattern} ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$; exploratory, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Fine-tuning extends gains to generators with SC up to ${\sim}$72\% (Appendix~\ref{app:finetuned-nli}). Cross-model LLM voting degrades strong generators while NLI never does; structural independence drives the escape (Appendices~\ref{app:cross-model-ablation},~\ref{app:drc}).

### Tool result

_source line 7777 · 2026-05-25T12:30:04.090746+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	\looseness=-1
5	Formal verification's value depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. SICA reduces to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape; symbolic confirmation bias holds across nine models (7B to frontier). Three lightweight diagnostics predict failure from a $K{=}12$ pilot (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
6	
7	\looseness=-1
8	For the broader neuro-symbolic community, our results expose a structural limitation of self-extraction pipelines: when the same model generates both reasoning and formal constraints, the symbolic layer inherits the model's distributional prior rather than providing an independent check. This limitation is not specific to logical constraint extraction; it applies to any self-verification paradigm where the verifier shares the generator's knowledge boundary \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}.
9	
10	\looseness=-1
11	Cross-architecture verification escapes only when the verifier exceeds generator accuracy (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); this points to \emph{architectural diversity} over scale as the key design principle. Trained verifiers---PRM \citep{lightman2024prm,wang2024mathshepherd}, ORM \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}---succeed precisely because they break the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ chain with independently supervised signal; our NLI escape instantiates this principle without task-specific training ($+6.67$\,pp). For test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}, our findings imply that allocating additional inference budget to self-consistent sampling yields diminishing returns once $K_\text{eff}$ saturates; redirecting compute toward structurally independent verification offers a more efficient path. Lightweight trained verification is a natural next step (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
12

[Director 指令] **Introduction 层级重构**

重写 introduction.tex 的 ¶2-¶4（¶1 和 Figure 1 环境不动，Contributions list 不动）。核心原则：¶2 是论点声明 → ¶3 是证据深度 → ¶4 是推论和实践意义。注意语言严谨性，不要用过于绝对的表述。

### ¶2：Core claim（合并当前¶2+¶3，精简为论点声明）

结构：测试 → 负面结果 → 原因（SCB）→ 形式化（一句话）

要点：
- 介绍 SICA（一句，不展开技术细节）
- 跨 6 模型 4 benchmark：SICA 在绝大多数问题上等同 SC，无显著正效果
- 核心诊断：问题不在 solver 而在 constraints——self-extracted constraints 系统性地支持多数答案（symbolic confirmation bias）
- 因为每条 trace 同时决定了 answer 和 constraints，形式验证通道退化为多数投票的加权回声（Observation 1）
- 保留 `\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}` 术语定义和 §5.1 引用
- **删除**当前¶3 中的 prior work 句（"Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct..."），移到¶3

注意：
- "vast majority of problems" 如果有具体数字（如 >90%）就用数字，否则用 "most problems"
- "reduces to" 要加 "approximately" 或用 Observation 1 的精确表述

### ¶3：Evidence depth（支撑核心 claim）

结构：泛化范围 → 修复失败 → independence-accuracy trade-off → 与 prior work 的联系

要点：
- SCB 跨 9 models（7B to frontier）成立
- 27 种 training-free remediation 跨 4 个类别全部 neutral-to-negative
- 打破 correlation 的方法破坏 accuracy；保留 accuracy 的方法无法实现 independence——persistent trade-off
- 与 self-correction 文献的联系：prior work 表明 LLM 无法自我纠正推理（Huang, Kamoi）；我们的分析为这些发现提供了具体的机制解释
- **不要**在这里提 cross-arch 或 diagnostics——那些是推论，放¶4

### ¶4：Implications（核心 claim 的推论）

结构：瓶颈定位 → escape 证据 → 实践工具

要点：
- 瓶颈在 self-extraction 本身，不在 solver 能力
- Cross-architecture verification 提供 preliminary evidence（$n=6$，exploratory）：当 verifier accuracy 超过 generator 时产生显著增益
- Lightweight diagnostics 从 pilot run 预测失败模式
- 每句话都要有适当 hedging（preliminary、exploratory 等）

### 语言严谨性检查清单
- 不用 "prove/proof"（我们是 empirical evidence）
- 不用 "always/never/all"（除非真的覆盖全部）
- 不用 "fundamental"（已改 persistent）
- "reduces to" 加限定（approximately / under the degeneration condition）
- cross-arch 结果必须标 preliminary/exploratory
- 不用 AI 风格的 -ing 尾句

完成后汇报新 Introduction 结构和字数。

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces, as in neuro-symbolic approaches \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}, and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\begin{figure*}[t!]
7	\centering
8	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}
9	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
10	\label{fig:pipeline}
11	\end{figure*}
12	
13	\looseness=-1
14	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces, deduplicates them, and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
15	
16	\looseness=-1
17	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority a

No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a robust empirical regularity of zero-shot self-extraction pipelines we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is robust (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis reveals a deeper mechanism underlying these findings; we provide a Markov chain formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTwenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is persistent (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \\citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence, a bottleneck also faced by test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by an exploratory capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three lightweight diagnostics derived from a pilot run enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces, deduplicates them, and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on most problems; no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). The failure lies not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on: self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a robust empirical regularity we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), distinct from trace-level unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2023language, wan2025unveiling}. Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal verification channel approximately reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote under the degeneration condition (Observation~1).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThis bias is not confined to a single model or task. Symbolic confirmation bias holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, and 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories---prompt diversity, constraint filtering, multi-agent debate, and cross-model extraction---all produce neutral-to-negative results (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). A persistent independence--accuracy trade-off emerges: methods that break trace correlation (e.g., multi-agent debate; \\citealp{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}) destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence. Prior work demonstrates that LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kamoi2024selfcorrect}; our analysis provides a concrete mechanism for these findings via the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain, where constraints are downstream of the same generative process that produces the answers they are meant to check.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe bottleneck lies in self-extraction itself, not in solver capability or model scale. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape ($n{=}6$ generator--verifier pairs, exploratory): a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). This result parallels test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}, where additional inference budget faces diminishing returns once effective ensemble diversity saturates. Three lightweight diagnostics derived from a pilot run enable practitioners to predict a priori whether a self-extraction-based aggregation method will improve over SC (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework})."
}

_source line 7799 · 2026-05-25T12:45:47.116383+00:00_

24:\item \textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the underlying mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \S\ref{sec:remediation}).

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}, and correlated LLM errors \citep{kim2025correlated} reduce the effective ensemble size \citep{dietterich2000ensemble}. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces, as in neuro-symbolic approaches \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm}, and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\begin{figure*}[t!]
7	\centering
8	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig1_pipeline_v8.pdf}
9	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
10	\label{fig:pipeline}
11	\end{figure*}
12	
13	\looseness=-1
14	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces, deduplicates them, and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). Across six models (7B--32B), four benchmarks, and multi-seed replication, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on most problems; no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). The failure lies not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on: self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a robust empirical regularity w

14	\smallskip\noindent
15	\textit{Empirical support.}\;%
16	Z3 rejection rate $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}|/|\mathcal{C}| \approx 0.034$ confirms $|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$. Per-trace yield averages $5.6$--$6.0$ constraints across answer groups (CV${=}0.26$; Appendix~\ref{app:yield-cv}); weight is thus proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote agree on 96.1\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\% across all conditions. Per-trace separation analysis confirms the extraction function is neutral---constraint density and quality are balanced across answer groups---and source-attribution scoring is the sole mechanism transmitting CoT correlation into verification (Appendix~\ref{app:per-trace-separation}).
17	
18	\label{sec:process-kappa}

_source line 7811 · 2026-05-25T12:49:54.488583+00:00_

50	
51	\looseness=-1
52	Cross-architecture verification escapes only when the verifier exceeds generator accuracy (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); this points to \emph{architectural diversity} over scale as the key design principle. Trained verifiers---PRM \citep{lightman2024prm,wang2024mathshepherd}, ORM \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}---succeed precisely because they break the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ chain with independently supervised signal; our NLI escape instantiates this principle without task-specific training ($+6.67$\,pp). For test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}, our findings imply that allocating additional inference budget to self-consistent sampling yields diminishing returns once $K_\text{eff}$ saturates; redirecting compute toward structurally independent verification offers a more efficient path. Lightweight trained verification is a natural next step (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
53
